ParlaMint-AT_2011-10-19-024-XXIV-NRSITZ-00124_d8e5622	Mr President of the Federal Republic of Germany! Madam President. Federal Chancellor! Mr. Vice-Chancellor! Ladies and gentlemen on the government bank and dear spectators on the ranks and in front of the screens! The last years were like a stormy low, a rough wind with violent turbulence and uncertainty. Many people are afraid: They are no longer quite sure whether their savings are still worth something tomorrow. As your Minister of Finance, ladies and gentlemen, I have a goal: I want to create stability so that our money retains its value. [[Congratulations with the ÖVP and with representatives of the SPÖ.]] [[Abg. Ing. Westenthaler: This is 42 pages now!]] Stable finances for a secure future, that is the motto of my work. High house! The pleite of the Investment Bank Lehman Brothers in September 2008 has radically changed the world economy overnight. The crisis of the US mortgage credit market and its innovative risks exported to all over the world have brought the literally “border growth” and stability out of balance. Massive irritations on international stock exchanges and financial markets have triggered a global recession. This led to a loss of confidence in particular. Until then, cheap money was available for everyone – for states, for banks, for businesses and for private. Suddenly, however, capital on the markets is completely closed. Through a determined approach at global, European and national level, the international community was able to restore confidence and stability in financial markets at the time. This has succeeded in ensuring security for the savings of citizens and for our prosperity. In Austria, a group of measures was adopted by the unanimous decision in Parliament at that time. A total of more than €100 billion of capital and liabilities have been made available for the security of savings deposits, more than 3 billion for business packages and more than 3 billion for tax relief for Austrians. These measures, ladies and gentlemen, were absolutely right. [[Congratulations with the ÖVP and with representatives of the SPÖ.]] At the time, they have achieved all their objectives, namely to safeguard jobs and thus purchasing power, to safeguard the location and prevent larger business insolvencies, to secure savings and stability in the financial market and to ensure new growth after the crisis. It has probably been a great merit in policy that due to proper measures the damage caused by the crisis was kept as low as possible in our country at that time – 2008. However, these measures have cost a lot of money. The budget deficit in Austria rose from around 2.6 billion in 2008 to 13 billion in 2010. Government debt increased by more than 25 billion through the cumulative deficits of 2008, 2009 and 2010. Austria has been able to cope well with these challenges through a prudent budgetary policy – other states are not. In the middle of the new growth of 2010 and 2011, the debt crisis of individual states has once again weakened this stability, as after the financial market crisis had apparently been overcome, the structural weaknesses of individual states have become apparent. Just as the financial economy has been on the verge of collapse a year and a half earlier, Greece in particular has had to experience that there is no state to do with excessive deficits and debt. [[Congratulations on the ÖVP.]] [[Abg. May. Stadler: Tired applause at the ÖVP!]] Expenditure has been galloped out and revenue has been avoided due to a somewhat competitive economy and an inefficient administration. Government debt of more than 150 percent of the annual economic performance, gross domestic product, has a dramatic need for action. This has once again lost confidence and the ability to refinance the markets. The euro area Member States therefore responded in May 2010 and launched a rescue shield – this time not for banks but for a state. [[Abg. Kickl: Ah, that has nothing to do with each other?- Oh, my God!]] With an effective volume of €440 billion for the European Stability Facility and the planned establishment of a permanent European Stability Mechanism, what has worked in the financial and banking crisis should also work in the debt crisis of a state. In addition to Greece, Portugal and Ireland have now also had to take European aid measures. To date, Austria has spent some 1.4 billion bilateral loans on Greece and has provided liabilities to the European Stability Facility of up to nearly 22 billion. [[Abg. Grosz: That's all a business!]] These have only been used a small part so far, and we are currently discussing at EU level the need to establish the EFSF infrastructure more flexible and efficient. Austria has mastered all these challenges through a prudent budget policy and a healthy economic base. [[Congratulations with ÖVP and SPÖ.]] [[Between call of the Abg. Ing. Westenthaler.]] Ladies and gentlemen, ladies and gentlemen! We will be faced with serious measures in the coming weeks and months due to the debt crisis in many European countries. For the next year, the economic researchers are unfortunately pessimistic. They have strongly revised their predictions for growth, yet – nevertheless – we will keep the course and will not leave the consolidation path. We have planned a stable budget. [[Congratulations on the ÖVP.]] The Economic Research Institute expects only 0.8 percent growth in 2012, and the Institute for Higher Studies predicts growth of 1.3 percent. [[Abg. May. Kogler: This takes up to 11 a.m. if you don't read faster!]] The new budgetary law helps us decisively. You have decided unanimously here, this new budgetary law pursues a very practical purpose. [[Between call of the Abg. Ing. Westenthaler.]] It is the backbone of budgetary policy and serves as a stable and reliable pillar, especially in times like this. The medium-term federal financial framework, the Federal Financial Framework Act, is a central contribution to saving. We therefore have a stable framework for four years, transparent for all departments, but also for the capital market and, in particular, for taxpayers. For all, it is clear at a glance how our expenditure policy in the coming years looks at how many resources are available per department and how the state's financial situation develops. This expenditure discipline on the financial framework is the first step towards curbing our deficits and as a debt brake. Strengthened by a constitutional provision – the proposal by the Vice-President and I am aware of it – we would then have a real, strong duty to reduce debt without losing sight of growth. [[Congratulations on the ÖVP.]] We are reducing our deficits faster than planned. However, we do not want a full brake, because that blocks the wheels of the economic engine. [[Ironic hustle and bustle at BZÖ.]] [[Abg. May. Stadler: A real intellectual!]] Therefore, in order to be able to counter economic downturns, especially with regard to the labour market, there are variable spending limits in certain areas of budgetary law. This means that in bad times more, in good times less is spent. In doing so, the Federal Financial Framework supports economic development and acts anticyclically – and this has proved to be the case. [[Congratulations with the ÖVP and with representatives of the SPÖ.]] A major renewal and speciality of budgetary law is the possibility of setting up reserves. Non-exploited resources from the departments do not fall back to the Minister of Finance and thus to the budget, but can be saved in the respective department and expenditure can be carefully and flexibly planned. This new provision also has a large savings effect, now about € 1 billion in reserves, as reserves. [[Call BZÖ: Where?]] This means, for ministers, that these reserves are available to the resorts in the following years, or that other reserves are made. For the Minister of Finance, this means that this money has not been spent so that no debts have been made and interest has been saved – therefore a positive thing. [[Congratulations with the ÖVP and with representatives of the SPÖ.]] The reform of budgetary law is now in the first phase of implementation and already a success model. Implementation with step two is then carried out in the 2013 budget and has another practical objective. The introduction of efficiency orientation with clearly defined objectives will make it even easier and more transparent in the future, why and for what we spend tax money. Generally speaking, the objective of the budgetary reform is to establish the budget as a comprehensive, integrated governance instrument for both resources and effects and performance. This model, developed by Austria, ladies and gentlemen, is now also the best-practice model for the EU and is to be applied by all Member States in the future. This has already been decided in the Six Pack Directive. [[Congratulations with the ÖVP and with representatives of the SPÖ.]] High house! My venerations Ladies and gentlemen! More than half of all new entrances to the pension are either early or invalidity pensions. We all need to be clear about the fact that this is not manageable in the long term. In 1970, people aged 62 were retired, and then they took up retirement for an average of 13 years. Now, my dear. Ladies and gentlemen, people on average retire at 59 years of age, i.e. three years earlier than 1970, although life expectancy has increased dramatically. The pensioner or the pensioner of today is then on average 22 years in pension. My venerations Ladies and gentlemen! The social partners' proposals to reduce early retirement are going in the right direction, and we will endeavour to implement them as far as possible. In addition to the savings to which all sectors of resources and expenditure contribute, the efficiency increases in the administration also come with respect to the expenditure laid down by law, as well as on discretion expenditure, personnel expenditure, physical expenditure and transfers. ParlaMint-AT_2011-10-19-024-XXIV-NRSITZ-00124_d8e8391	Next speaker: Mr Ing. Lugar. - Please.
ParlaMint-AT_2011-10-19-024-XXIV-NRSITZ-00124_d8e8412	Mr President. High house! Mrs Finanzminister, you also said in your speech that Austria has a negative primary balance. This probably sounds relatively harmless for many here in the hall and for many in front of the TV screen. What is it, a negative primary balance? I have made the effort to turn this into a “normal” borrower. A “normal” person takes a credit and pays, just as it used to be, the interest plus a repayment. That is to say, one pays the interest that is being paid, and it is always repaid by the amount outstanding, and after ten, 15, 20 years one has paid this loan back. That's the normal case. Now there is a second variant, which is already somewhat worrying: It remains the credit amount. One picks up – house number – €200 000 and pays only interest every year. The 200 000 € credit remains ten, 15, 20 years, and one hopes that in 20 years a lot will come of it. It's a little more critical. Now, however, there is neither a debt spiral – the third variant – nor a worse one, namely that someone takes up a loan, but does not repay both capital and interest. This is, as it were, the worst thing that can happen to you if you think. But it's worse! Even worse is what the Austrian state is doing, and this is hidden behind this “negative primary balance”: The State cannot repay its loan, nor can it serve the interest rate, nor can it manage with its money, so it must, as it were, receive additional loans for the daily expenditure. This means that not only interest will not be paid, but additional loans will be received – not only for interest, but also for the normal budget, and the loans will not be used. That's it! And if you want to believe your words, Mrs Minister, you have to say that Greece is better in this regard. Greece has no primary negative balance. That is, if Greece does not have to pay interest, it could challenge its normal budget, without a deficit – Austria cannot. That is, we are even worse in this matter than the Greeks are. [[Abg. Silhavy: You can overdo yourself!]] And now the question is: Why don't we do anything? President Moser of the Court of Auditors is someone who, I believe, is slowly desperate in this House. Mr Moser – I know that since I was in this House, for three years – has repeatedly exhorted, exhorted, exhorted. Mr Moser said again and again in every budget speech, with every budget template: We must finally introduce reforms! And what happened? - Nothing. And it is also this time again – we have heard Mrs. Minister's budget speech – that: In this speech, the very important positions – such as pensions, such as health, such as administration – have been addressed as important points, and one has to do something, but there is nothing specific about this budget speech. The Minister has once again failed to make concrete announcements on how to save, for example, the pension system. We know we're on average going to pension with 58. In the OECD comparison, we are only marginally better than Luxembourg, all others go to retirement later. That means we're world champions at the early pensions. And at the ÖBB still people with 52 in early retirement. [[Abg. Haberzettl: Bullshit!]] – These are the current numbers! With the ÖBB the average age of retirement is 52, you have to imagine that! ParlaMint-AT_2011-10-19-024-XXIV-NRSITZ-00124_d8e8887	Mr President. Thank you, Mrs. Federal Minister! Mr. State Secretary! High house! The Court of Auditors shall have the task of preparing the annual accounts of the Federal Republic of Germany and of checking how the budget has been carried out, including reference to the framework conditions. And these framework conditions show, when you look at the 2010 Federal Accounting Accounting Office, that It is important to ensure that structural reforms are taken into account in order to comply with the legal requirements and the rules that are laid down. Article 13(1) 2 of the Federal Constitution provides that all – federal government, countries, municipalities – strive to achieve a macroeconomic balance and sustainable budgets while also coordinating their objectives in this regard. The stability programme 2011-2015, which you did on 27 July 2015. In April 2011, it was agreed, among other things – and this was also mentioned today in the budget speech of the Federal Minister – that a sustainable budget and financial policy, a balanced budget on the economic cycle, a common budgetary responsibility of the local authorities and, at the same time, a sustainable budget consolidation. Considering the data included in the financial statements and taking into account the financial crisis at the same time, it must be pointed out that the financial crisis has significantly deteriorated the budgetary figures, but even before the financial crisis, namely in 2006, it was already visible that Austria needed a structural reform. I would like to remind you that in 2006, we had a real growth of 3.7 percent, excellent data on the current account balance, the unemployment rate, the increase in the number of employed persons employed. Even in the years 2006 and 2007, we did not manage to balance the budget. In the meantime, the financial crisis has made this problem worse. This has led to the urgent need to tackle the reforms. We had better economic data in 2010 and they were also envisaged for 2011. Despite this, the deficit is increasing, regardless of the debt level. If the net financial debt of 2007 is compared to that of 2010, they have increased by 29.4 billion. It has increased total government debt by 40 billion, the liabilities by 40 billion. The liability alone amounts to € 129 billion, as can be seen from the federal accounts. In 2009 and 2010 alone, total government debt increased by €14 billion, with net financial debt by €9 billion. That is to say, measures are needed, even if we consider the budget data that Maastricht data provides. It has already been mentioned several times today that the budget deficit is €13.2 billion – which accounts for 4.6 percent of GDP – and that government financial debt is 71.8 percent. However, with regard to general government budget responsibility, account must also be taken of the fact that the debt of the countries has also increased greatly, from 2006 to 2010 from 8.5 billion to 16.4 billion euros, and the share of government debt has thus increased from 5.3 percent to 8%. If you look at the communities, you can see that debt has also increased sharply, from 4,9 to 8 billion €, which at the same time shows that the communities are in a very precarious situation. This also shows the 2010 municipal financial report for 2009, where a structural deficit for municipalities of €502 million has been identified, and at the same time, that the free financial peak necessary to carry out investments has fallen from €549 million in 2008 to €7 million in 2009 and will continue to fall to €260 million. ==References== The importance of reforms also shows that communities alone have had an increase of 8.8 percent in 2009 in the field of social welfare and 6.6 percent in the field of health. The federal accounts show that we urgently need structural reforms, we want to achieve precisely the planned macroeconomic balance, sustainable budgets and, at the same time, sustainable budget consolidation. However, this is not only a fact which the Court of Auditors alone points out, but also a fact which, in June and July of this year, the ECOFIN and at the same time the IMF indicated that measures must be taken in the light of structural reforms, in terms of bringing together expenditure and financing responsibilities. The figures of the 2012 draft budget, including taking into account the federal financial framework, show that, if the reforms are not implemented, expenditure related to the past will consume expenditure related to the future. The success in 2010 compared with the planned data for 2015 shows that approximately 90 percent of the planned €8.2 billion will be spent on pensions and interest. This would result in us spending 40 percent of the total expenditure on pensions and interest rates in 2015, i.e. 60 percent of net tax revenue for this purpose. In the years 2005 to 2010, we still had an increase of 12.3 percent in pensions and interest rates. This was lower than GDP growth of 16.7 percent. If you look at the years 2010 to 2015, then you will see that there are likely to be pensions and interest rates, as they are planned to rise by 32.4 percent, but GDP is only 21.3 percent. That is, we need action in this area. If you look at the federal financial framework by 2015 you can see that – although the last years were affected by the financial crisis – if you take the federal financial framework, a new debt of €53 billion is planned from 2010 to 2015. This is €5 billion higher than the debt from 2005 to 2010, with the corresponding effect of the financial crisis. This also shows that the balanced budget will be difficult or impossible to achieve over the economic cycle, and the European Commission's requirements also depend on a structural deficit of 0 to 0.5 percent. This means that the acceptable permanent re-indebtedness will be exceeded by 5 to 9 billion per year between 2011 and 2014. The structural deficit in 2011 is expected to be 2.8 percent and will continue to be 1.9 percent in 2014. So we have to do homework in order to make the administration more efficient and lead the money where it is needed, that is to say, to the citizen. We need action due to historical causes in the field of education, health, accounting and pension systems. In this case, we also need an accounting system, which is meaningful, an accounting system that gives us the opportunity to control the whole of the federal, state and local authorities. The current accounting system of the countries and municipalities does not offer this possibility. We have no faithful representation of the actual wealth or the the financial situation of the local authorities, the lack of sufficient clarity by the camera list and the lack of comparability of the individual accounts. We need more clarity and transparency in order to be able to control – including in countries and communities – by making comparability with the federal government in terms of data bases, checking and booking practice. We need a description of the true financial situation, including the results of the distributed units. We need a completeness of the accounting system in which the burdens and future financial years – including leasing, special financing and housing support – are also highlighted. The Federal Minister mentioned that the budgetary system introduced by Austria to the federal government is a benchmark for the EU. But unfortunately, this benchmark is not sufficient to actually introduce this to the local authorities in Austria, in countries and in communities, but which would be necessary, one really wants to manage overall. The fact that this is currently limited also shows the medium-term planning that we have at present, which, even if one takes the countries and the federal government, is not sufficient both from the information content, from the period and from the objectives which differ from one another. We have no consistent, rolling planning, no in-depth coordination, and the medium-term plans are not included in the stability programme. The overall control, as provided for in the Federal Constitution, is therefore not sufficient at present. So what do we need? – We need planning documents within the framework of the annual roll-over and a planning horizon, which is in line with the Federal Financial Framework Act and defines Austria-wide minimum requirements in order at the same time to put in place territorial bodies across those measures necessary to achieve precisely the objectives set out in the Stability Programme and in the Federal Constitution, namely a macroeconomic balance and sustainable budgets, which is certainly not sufficiently guaranteed without structural reforms. - Thank you.

[[Abg. Dipl.-Ing. Deimek: Change! 5 minutes in there, and it's changed!]] [[Abg. Petzner: What happened? Nothing!]] We now know that you do not like this, Mr Petzner. [[Congratulations on the ÖVP.]] Let me also talk about the Financial Services Act! Mr Pilz said that I could read, so I read the motion for a resolution to which the Federal Government is asked to better regulate the financial service providers and the securities supervisory law: ‘Development proposal,The Federal Government is invited to submit to the National Council a proposal for a law which will fully reform the system of investment advisory professions, in particular the professional image of the financial service assistant according to (...) Securities Supervision Act 2007 (...) iVm (...) Commercial Regulation 1994 (...), which can in any case lead to the removal.’ – That was the task. In accordance with this mandate, the problem situation and the possible solutions were evaluated and a corresponding draft law was sent here. The evaluation of the improvements in this profession took place on the basis of the following parameters: 1. best possible improvement of the quality of advice for customers, 2. Strengthening the responsibility of the concession carriers, 3. Legal security for customers, 4. Constitutional and EU legislation, 5. Supervisory efficiency, 6th social and employment aspects.’ This was the result of this draft. The profession of the securities intermediary as a regulated trade with its own professional status and special training, comparable to that of the asset advisor, with the work-related adjustments and ongoing continuing training for sales on behalf of investment service companies and investment firms was thus redefined. Because of the parallel necessary new regulation of the Securities Supervision Act 2007 and the Trade Regulation 1994 a joint action was necessary between the Ministry of Economic Affairs and the Ministry of Finance. In In the course of the review procedure, it was then considered that the securities transfer should be granted to banks and insurance companies as masters of business. This variant, however, was not a majority. Securities intermediaries can therefore only operate in the future for concessiond investment firms and investment service companies. All in all, an intermediary may only operate for three concessiond masters of business. This will create transparency for customers. I believe that we have not only been able to read the motion for a resolution but have also implemented it accordingly. However, in order to set strict standards in order to avoid damage to those persons who invest or are advised, I am willing to cooperate if Parliament has any further details on the products or to other professions or in combination with other professions. The expertise of our House is of course available to Parliament.

The service and network neutrality is also very important and also in our principle paper for mobile phones or even Internet services. What is also very important is corresponding catastrophic guidance. This has already been discussed in the Committee. It may now require the regulatory authority from the respective operators that non-existent documents be supplemented in the course of an investment project, an extension and so on and are in the final effect. The main point of this law, however, was consumer rights. There have been a lot of complaints on the part of consumers over the past few years about the various mobile operators. One of them we were able to bring to the public through our website “www.unzensuriert.at”. With this law a great bar is now pushed against some of these painters. For example, there will be better information. In the case of some mobile operators, it has been the case since the summer that, with 80 per cent of the Volumes receive a corresponding SMS warning. Or: Something very important are paper bills, especially for older people or companies. One now has mandatory paper accounts and can either opt for an electronic invoice. Further innovations for consumers are a service block for data and similar or contract maximisation at 24 months. We are absolutely in favour of this law and the corresponding regulation. Some of the things requested by the BZÖ in a resolution are clarified in this regulation, and we also believe that this regulation is quite sufficient. Why are we also for the rest of the law? – We are in Austria at the rear of the broadband initiative, and we believe that this will allow us to make a proper boost. On question 6 concerning the New Secondary School: The shift of the school career decision from the 10th to the 14th. Year of life, as you know, is an important goal that we have always supported, and the Federal Government has financed this comprehensive expansion of the New Middle School, which I have already mentioned – that is, not a pilot project here and there, the stage is long over; we want to finance the comprehensive expansion – with additional €61.1 million for 2012. The corresponding novel for the acquisition of the New Secondary School from the school to the regular school system is in preparation and, as Mrs Minister tells me, I believe I can also say this together with the coalition partner's negotiator, which was sent to Parliament before Christmas. So here we are almost in the final phase also in terms of the regular education system and the corresponding novels. On question 7 on the right of employment and remuneration: As I have just mentioned, it has also been agreed, at the government clause, to present this new right of service by the summer of 2012. The working groups on this issue are available. Yes, I'll tell you the following: Of course, this negotiation is one that takes more time than is likely to be the case in other similar areas. This is also strongly linked to the fact that the employee representatives' arguments go a little further than the time of their presence to discuss, but it was the question of the equipment of schools and the possibility of having the job at school, it was these arguments that naturally extended this discussion in the working groups. However, I am convinced that, despite the complexity of the debate, we will come to a clear conclusion by the summer of 2012 and that we will be able to shape the working conditions accordingly and still achieve a higher commitment for newly started teachers directly at school, otherwise the implementation of all day school forms would also encounter long-term financing and organisational problems. So it's true that's gonna take longer. I just wanted to tell you why the discussion in this context has expanded somewhat, especially in the direction of working conditions of teachers. The Federal Government, of course, is trying to deal with this – as in other issues – with social partnership. Of course, one could ask purely legal and theoretically what one needs to negotiate with someone at all, but I believe that this up-to-date social partnership with one another in Austria is an asset that we very much appreciate on other issues and that is therefore also to be lived in this case. Question 9 on autonomy in schools: With the new law in spring 2011, the tasks of the headmasters were laid down by law. This is the responsibility of the management at the school location. Staff development, the development of school profiles and the increased participation of teachers in the selection of the teacher team are already in existence in individual federal states and are good results, as Mrs Minister has also done. The profile education and educational focusses at school locations are already possible and lived practice within the framework of school autonomy. Question 10: This is an important point, since, particularly as regards the question of competence, he has already in the past led to fierce debates in our federalist system between the question of where teachers belong and all the extremes we have experienced in the debate. – There has been an attitude here that I believe is in principle correct, namely that there is a central responsibility of the federal government, but that at the same time there are two-tracks to be eliminated. In five of nine national school councils, national teachers are already being executed by the Council of State Schools, which in this case means the elimination of double tracks. We have therefore moved away from the debate, where they are to be employed – medium-sized/immediate federal administration – to the problem that if you want to eliminate any double-trackness in the administration, you must also, of course, make it possible. Since there is already this in five of the nine national school councils and the other federal states are also invited to carry out this efficiency improvement, it is necessary to make a clarification in the federal constitution so that this is also secured for those who already do it and for those whom we invite to see this possibility in the future. This is in preparation and will also play a role in further discussions with the federal states. On the question of 11, cooperation between schools at secondary and tertiary education institutions: This concerns cooperation, which is already taking place in different ways and depending on regional priorities. These are particularly pronounced in higher education. The new mature and diploma exam will also be developed in close cooperation with the universities and improve the transition from school to university. On the question 12, budgetary objectives of the tertiary sector: In the government programme, the 2-percent share of GDP – which is meant to be up to 2020 – is anchored in the tertiary sector. Austria's investment in tertiary education is already close to the OECD section, so it is still expanding, and is 1.3 percent in Austria. [[Abg. Dr. Glavischnig-Piesczek: That's close?! 1.3 to 2% is close?- Oh, my God!]] The increase to 2 percent remains our goal, and it is also necessary to further promote more and better trained university graduates. That's what I'm coming back to. It is important to have an efficient structure and a high-quality framework in the higher education plan that is being developed. This brings me seamlessly to the questions 13 to 16: There is no agreement for the introduction of study contributions in the government programme. That is why this is a discussion you know that we do not yet have a common view on this, but that there are different views. However, I share – and I would like to stress this over and over again – the opinion of those who say that we already have to mention the question of reforming universities, the university plan, the provision of these one billion over three years, that the study contributions that have been withdrawn in the past – a little more than €150 million – will be made available to universities from the general budget by 2013. I must therefore always mention this because some give the impression that the money is missing at the universities at an hour. It is not missing at the universities, but the political discussion is whether it should be paid out of the general tax pot or whether it would be reasonable for the individual to pay these tuition fees. Here are the well-known positions that have something to do with social security, so that how the ratio of students to scholarships is, how tax revenue can be assessed as a whole, and whether these contributions should be recovered from the general budget to the universities. This is important in the debate because the discussion often confronts the lack of university contributions. That is why you have to stress this again and again in all honesty. The European Commission calls on the EU 2020 strategy to make higher education more attractive for wider classes of society, in particular for disadvantaged and socially weak groups. In other words, we have actually committed ourselves to greater social justice and to providing all social classes with the opportunity to study at universities. This, of course, must also be taken seriously in this debate and included in it. Every single graduate – I don't have statistics now, whether there are five exceptions, but in general this is the case – is a profit for the taxpayer and brings more in the long term, because he has a higher employment rate, a higher tax revenue. This is why university graduates are of course also an advantage for society in the long term. Therefore, the conditions at universities, the university plan and the needs are rightly addressed, because this is an objective which is important not only in the European Union but, of course, for every country, including Austria, for the future of the country. That is why I would like to refer once again to this discussion in connection with the preparation of the new university plan. It is also true that by 2012 additional offensive funds of €80 million will be used for the support and relation of teachers to the students in order to also demand efficiency increases or contributions from universities towards efficiency gains. On the question of 17 on budgetary resources for research and development: The Austrian universities receive 1.36 billion € from the budgeted research resources. According to the 2012 budget, the Science Research Fund receives almost € 139 million in federal funding. The universities have three-year global budgets, and the global budgets for 2013 to 2015 are currently being negotiated with the BMF regarding the next BFRG. As regards the development of career models in research, technology and innovation, there are funding programmes with the different ministries, including with the BMVIT. 5 600 research jobs in research firms are directly funded. However, I would also like to take this opportunity, especially in this debate, of those responsible members of the government, but also many of you who, in all these results, in all these processes, have been involved in higher education priorities in the past, which have been negotiated, have brought results, but are also involved to further improve education, for one thing is completely undisputed for me: If we want to improve the competitiveness of our country or if we are discussing the competitiveness of the European Union – tomorrow, I think, again in the urgent debate on the issue of the euro or other issues – then a very essential point in terms of competitiveness is the question of education. ParlaMint-AT_2011-10-19-024-XXIV-NRSITZ-00124_d8e17739	Mr President. Mrs. Minister! Ladies and gentlemen, I will comment on the Gas Economic Act, which, from our point of view, contains a lot of positive things, such as strengthening consumers, households and commercial businesses. It facilitates the change of suppliers and, indeed, a greater separation, the so-called unbundling, between network operators and gas supply operators. But this strengthening on the one hand – and here I am already on the main point – is actually a very essential attack on property ownership of property owners, namely that land owners can also be expropriated for new gas infrastructure if this gas infrastructure is in public interest. Now I wonder if I can also believe the Federal Government's ads that we want to become "energy self-sufficient" – I say, under quotations: Then why is additional gas infrastructure in the public interest? I'd ask you to explain that. And if gas infrastructure is already in place, why do we not find priority access for biogas in this law, as is the case in Germany? In Austria it is still the case that, in doubt, fossil gas will have priority. It doesn't really go together. In Austria, taxpayers spend €2 billion on gas imports a year, mainly going to Russia. I think why can't you just replace gas? It is already clear to me that we will need gas, especially for industrial applications, as a transitional technology. But why, if this is a transitional technology, is there public interest in additional lines? Why is there no targeted substitution of gas use in households, in heat generation, in electricity generation? Why not reduce these €2 billion? Why not substitute gas? And why, if already gas use, no priority use of biogas? That is absolutely incomprehensible. We cannot accept this law in this way. [[Congratulation with the greens.]] But what is particularly critical in this law is this so-called expropriation paragraph. Not only as I said, I do not understand that additional gas infrastructure is in public interest and that despite our catastrophic climate balance, property owners can be expropriated for this, but in particular democracy policy, because the main public interest is that gas infrastructure is inside the network’s long-term network plan. This is usually done by the control zone leaders. The gas companies are almost self-esteeming that this is in the public interest. I'm quite concerned about that. The amendment also does not change much of what the regulatory authority should confirm, because it is formulated in such a way that, if this is just in the plan, the regulatory authority has to confirm it. I find this democratic policy extremely worrying. As I said, additional gas infrastructure cannot be in the public interest. And if the companies can still exhibit it themselves and property owners can be expropriated, then this can by no means be contemporary. [[Congratulation with the greens.]] There are also already victims in Austria who have already been expropriated. ParlaMint-AT_2011-10-19-024-XXIV-NRSITZ-00124_d8e18821	Mr President. Ladies and gentlemen! I am pleased that we have today the Gas Economic Act for decision-making. It has been noted here that we would whip it through. Someone has already said before me that this is not the case, and I can only confirm it. After we did that on 23 March. Having decided in the Committee on March 2011 was enough time to discuss this, and this has happened over the last few weeks and months. There are several reasons why we should implement this law. One is a formal reason: that we have the third EU internal market energy liberalisation programme, which essentially also intends to take appropriate liberalisation steps in the gas sector. This is the formal background, where the time factor is that we should have had this in itself in March. The implementation within the framework of the other countries is quite similar to that of us, we have to say, because only nine countries have implemented it so far, and the others have now been invited – including us – to present what it has to do with the implementation, otherwise the EU will initiate a corresponding infringement procedure. This will not matter to some of you, but there are also beautiful and important backgrounds. One is that, well known – and therefore I do not understand the position of the Greens – Germany is out of the field of nuclear energy and that there are clear opinions and assessments, which include that we cannot guarantee security of supply in Europe with renewable energy alone and with energy efficiency, but that natural gas will be a very important bridge technology. [[Abg. May. Brunner: I said that!]] Yes, and for this bridge technology, as for the electricity, corresponding lines and networks are needed. That understands itself, otherwise it would be worthless. The fact that Austria could be a kind of energy hub, especially as regards the coverage of the road to Germany, is a second matter in this context. However, the fact that we are substantially forcing the unbundling that forces consumer rights is already a conclusion to the third important objective, and that is simply more competition. All of them have partially criticised in broadcasts that the price trend is not the way it could have been, that is to say, more competition, leading down and not up. We've seen this the same way. But if I want to develop this, I need the appropriate framework conditions. And we have these conditions. In order to relax, I would also like to say something about what the BZÖ has now said about consumer rights: What I or we say, that's what we hold. Therefore, as regards the change of suppliers, we now have much better conditions, similar to the ElWOG. In the future there will be three weeks, before it was six to eight weeks. [[Intrusion of the Abg. May. Widmann.]] - I'll be right back to the electronics. – In other words, there are savings of up to € 170 for a household. That is a very important factor in times like this. Now, Mr Widmann, we're coming to the electronic provider exchange. We need a corresponding electronic data platform. We are in the process of clarifying this, also with the E-Control, and there are still problems – this is true for the ElWOG sector and for the gas sector; we want to do both in a meaningful way – in identifying the electronic signature. But we will soon have solved them. We will then be able to do this together and, as we promised, we will probably be able to implement it in these months. So that which has been promised will be observed 1 : 1 and also in accordance with your motion for a resolution which you have referred to. In this context, we have also regulated a few other things that you have sometimes mounted. For example, there is a maximum price scheme for vulnerable customers. There is what the definition of the needy customer reaches, the regulation that small businesses can also be involved, which benefits them. While earlier for guarantees, advance payments could be required three instalments, there is now a rate. So the orientation is stronger and better in the direction of customer interest and also customer information. As with the ElWOG, we also have the statutory tariff calculator in the area. I believe that the second and perhaps most important point is the unbundling. We essentially have the ownership-law unbundling as a basic model, the independent network operator, the independent transmission system operator. Whether this is the ISO or the ITO system, I do not want to explain in detail due to the advanced time, but in the final effect there is a unbundling between producers and suppliers. This will also allow for greater competition in the interests of all of us. There is also a corresponding unbundling of distribution system operators. As for the gas trade – which has not yet been mentioned – the introduction of the so-called entry-exit market model takes place. That's much better. In the past, only the one that has also had the transmission capacity has been calculated, and now, in contrast, there will be an unhindered gas trade across the network and thus for other suppliers. That sounds unspectacular, but it is very important in detail. Nor has there been any such legal protection with regard to the tariffs, including the system. Now we have a move and better legal protection. We have a fixed specification similar to the ElWOG, which will lead to incentives, and also a network development plan. There is now the point that Mr Katzian has just mentioned, in connection with expropriation. It does not make any sense if you need and also have a bridge technology, but in the event of an emergency you cannot carry out the appropriate expropriations in the public interest. That the e-control not only creates the notice and says: There's a plan – just economic interest, and that's what the matter is!, it's right. But this is what Mr Katzian said earlier, namely: It will be fully evaluated, and inclusion will take place as early as the planning process. I have full confidence in the e-control and not in those who are interested in any other decisions. I do not doubt the objective decision here. So much confidence in a proven institution, I think, can be quite sure. I believe that this is a really good law. And quite honestly, Mr Widmann, the improvements you have made in terms of gas consumption, for example, are not the decisive factor. The crucial factor, however, is to focus on customer interests, to have more competition and secure network expansion plans. If you know what the European Commission is saying about this, you want to have very clear safeguards. We will deal with this carefully because we also need to involve civil rights. So in the final effect, I think, we will proceed carefully and comprehensively. In this context, I should like to thank Mr Hofer, who has already been addressed. As with several energy laws, he has brought in absolutely best expertise. I thank him and also expressly to the Freedom Party. We have made a direction-setting decision that will be used by the customer in Austria.

ParlaMint-AT_2011-10-19-024-XXIV-NRSITZ-00124_d8e29840	I'll call the 46. Agenda item. I would like to draw attention to the fact that there are only two statements of speech and then the vote. The first speech comes from Mr Magistrate. Donnerbauer. - Please.
ParlaMint-AT_2011-10-19-024-XXIV-NRSITZ-00124_d8e29867	Mr President. Mrs. Federal Minister! Ladies and gentlemen! I can do it briefly, it's a consensual matter. It is a question of the European Court of Justice on 24 October. In May 2011, a passport in the Austrian Notarial Act was recognised as incompatible with EU law, namely the nationality requirement for access to the profession of the notary. With this law, with this change of law, we are creating, as it were, an EU legal order by removing this requirement of nationality in the Notarial Code and in various other matters, but also clearly continuing the work of the Notaries as a public Define task. I am therefore seeking your approval. Thank you.
ParlaMint-AT_2011-10-19-024-XXIV-NRSITZ-00124_d8e29899	Next speaker: Mrs Mag. Mulder. - Please.
ParlaMint-AT_2011-10-19-024-XXIV-NRSITZ-00124_d8e29928	Ladies and gentlemen! The honourable Member has already mentioned the reason why we decide on this amendment. I think it is also important to consider the consequences of such an implementation. What effect does it have on the Austrian notarial system if we implement an ECJ judgment here today? – In the short term, it probably has no effect because the waiting lists for the notarial candidates are very, very long, but in the medium term there will be an improvement: Benefits from enhanced language competence in the notaries and familiarity with the foreign legal system. An example just: If an Austrian or an Austrian in Holland inherits a property or operates a legal business, there is in the future the possibility of taking a Dutch notary from Austria, who is familiar with language and legal system there – this is an advantage. For lawyers with us, on the other hand, there is the possibility of exercising the notary's profession throughout Europe in the future, so there are opportunities for rise. Thank you.

Point Report of the Committee on Labour and Social Affairs on the Government template (1408, e.g._Other Organiser Agreement between the Republic of Austria and the Republic of Moldova on social security (1426, e.g.)
ParlaMint-AT_2011-10-19-024-XXIV-NRSITZ-00124_d8e30008	We'll get to 47. Agenda item. I call Mr Karlsböck. - Please.
ParlaMint-AT_2011-10-19-024-XXIV-NRSITZ-00124_d8e30032	Mr President. Minister! Ladies and gentlemen! I'm going to keep this short now, too. The purpose of the present Agreement with Moldova is to ensure a wide range of protection in the field of pension insurance through the equal treatment of mutual nationals, the aggregation of periods of insurance for the acquisition of benefits. that the pension is guaranteed in accordance with the periods of insurance and the export of benefits in each Contracting State. In principle, social security agreements should be assessed positively. The present agreement also welcomes the aggregation of periods of insurance for the acquisition of benefits in pension insurance. However, the present agreement raises unexplained questions so that our consent cannot be given. The Agreement refers to the explicit request of the Republic of Moldova only in the field of pension insurance and the legislation to be applied in the case of cross-border employment. The question is why the Republic of Moldova does not also want to include health, accident and unemployment insurance, which are normally included in the social security agreements concluded with European States. No statement of reasons is given in the Government template. However, an overall package seemed much more meaningful to me. Furthermore, there is no information on how many Austrian citizens work in the Republic of Moldova. A cost statement for the Republic of Moldova would also be necessary in order to assess whether Austria is also benefiting from this agreement or whether we are creating only costs. Where is the principle of reciprocity? So why does Austria need this agreement? Too many questions are open. There are no data on what costs arise. Ladies and gentlemen! Unfortunately, the FPÖ Group cannot agree.

We discussed this in great detail in the Committee. It is a good proposal to ensure that employees are accountable for the benefits they provide in different countries, in the case of the two contracting countries. However, the agreement also offers employers and employers, Austrian companies who go to Moldova as investors, or also in the opposite case, the advantage that they have a clearer legal status and that workers in particular do not have to pay double insurance contributions. A good, clear government template for workers, but also for investors. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-AT_2011-10-19-024-XXIV-NRSITZ-00124_d8e31977	Mr President. Mr. State Secretary! Ladies and gentlemen! As a further outflow of the Lisbon Treaty, two issues are on the agenda today: the Rules of Procedure Act will be adapted here and an EU Information Act will be adopted. In fact, as with all these things, it is an employment therapy that is to be distracted from the fact that, as the State of Austria and also the Parliament, we are to be distracted as legislators, that sovereignty is restricted, and possibly, if so, even budgetary law is to be restricted shortly. It is an apparent democratization that is being enforced here, it is a apparent involvement of Parliaments with this subsidiarity review, which in fact is only a work or employment therapy. And now we have something new: a apparent improvement in the information and thus also the involvement of Parliaments. It seems to be because this information can already be obtained essentially now, apparently because it improves so great things, such as that the main committee can decide on a survey on EU issues or a single Member can make a request that it can be decided to bring an action before the ECJ for breach of the principle of subsidiarity. So incredible improvements that bring much more to the citizens and individual representatives of the region. Since we have just noticed that this is an apparent improvement, that the costs are completely unknown, that it is red tape – we have 60 pages of legislative text for this information improvement, which is supposed to come here for us – that the effort is enormous and therefore in reality is completely unclear what it is supposed to bring, we are not willing to carry this distraction maneuver, and therefore also our simple rejection.

So far, it has not been mandatory for a minister or the government to explain the Austrian position on certain projects; in the future, yes, we can request that. Then we'll see how this will be in practice. It will take a few months to deal with the new instruments. What we have forgotten in the Novelle Rules of Procedure is a veto right of a group of Members against such agendas as we do today; we may be able to do so in the next novel. But apart from that, there are new rights of interaction and of question, Mr Stefan, with very short time limits for reply. No longer with six weeks or eight weeks, or how long it takes for a written request, but very short response periods, also due to the experience of this year, where the events have been partially overlooked and one would not be used with an information after two months, in no way. So: Let's hope that this works, we'll fill it with life! This applies to all of us. This applies not only to the Members of the Standing Subcommittee or to the Members of the Main Committee, but to all of you, in principle in all committees, for some more, for some less. However, all committees will have to deal more with EU issues in the future than before. - Thank you.

The proposal by Stadler seemed quite useful to me. There are few changes needed, and infrastructure is needed. If you have the political will, you can do it. I think it would also trigger a rethink for various members of the public prosecutor's office if there were such a control institution once. Thank you.

ParlaMint-AT_2011-10-19-024-XXIV-NRSITZ-00124_d8e33932	Ladies and gentlemen! I think Mr Stadler's request is absolutely worthy of discussion. What would change and what would be interesting is the method of control. We now have the problem of asking questions and getting more or less appropriate answers, and that was it. The tension – and this also shows the subcommittee on the control of the BAT – is that one could, as it were, control the prosecutor's office in another way, sometimes also take note of the file and thus actually verify decisions of the prosecutor's office in a serious way. The essential point – and that is what we need to discuss – is that I believe that it should most likely be a check-up check, which takes place especially when procedures have been closed. What I don't want is for us to conduct in ongoing proceedings, because this could lead to increasing politicization, especially since there would be a risk that, of course, investigation strategies of the public prosecutor would also be disclosed if it was reported, so that it would be possible to get insight into proceedings through parties. We don't want all of that. We do not want to so to say designate the prosecutor's office in relation to its investigation strategies – that is the task of the Supreme Authorities – but we want to control whether the prosecutor's office has complied with the law of its activities. I would do that, especially if a procedure was set up or nothing happened and one had to control why it was so and if it was plausible. [[Abg. May. Stadler: Or nothing happens!]] But that is, I think, the sensitive point that we are making a clear decision: It is an inspective check that shows, at all events, maladministration. I think these details will be discussed, and in that sense I am looking forward to the debate in the Committee on Justice. I don't think the prosecutor's office should be afraid of it. No one can escape control, and as we know, this also has a preventive function: Control usually leads to more precise work. - Thank you.

ParlaMint-AT_2011-10-19-024-XXIV-NRSITZ-00124_d8e34041	Ladies and gentlemen! As you know, the ABGB has grown 200 years old this year. The definition of marriage has also grown to 200 years old. I am not disturbed that the word “zwey” is written with y and “beystand” is also written with y, because the texting is so old, but I believe that you have to be realistic and say: In 200 years the world has changed permanently! It is not to be seen that we believe that the definition of marriage does not have to change. I mean, the marriage definition still speaks of the inseparability of marriage; we know that 50 percent of marriages are divorced. It is not about whether we like it or not, but it is about offering adequate solutions when separations arise. The same is true of child production: The definition of the ABBB so to speak, requires child production as the purpose of marriage. Everyone is happy when there are children, but real is not the only purpose. No one forbids an older couple to marry if child production would not be possible at all. I therefore believe that we need a timely definition of marriage, that it is time after 200 years to think about a better definition. That is what we have done, and I would like to wrap this up with three sentences. It's primarily about a trust relationship and a comprehensive partnership on a long-term basis – what's more beautiful than two people want to do it for each other, no matter which sex, and no matter what motivation? This new definition would make sense. But it is not with the definition of marriage, but we also need a comprehensive reform of divorce law. In fact, we have to get away from the indebtedness, from the devastation decision. It can't be that by law we drive people into destructive, ruinous marital wars that don't benefit anyone. In so far as I think there are two steps to reform: One is a new definition of marriage, and the second is a reform of divorce law. - Thank you.
